We should aim to keep the same arithmetic order per element where possible to get identical results? For Cholesky, a right-looking algorithm differs in rounding order.\n\nBest approach: parallelize the original left-looking column algorithm. In the original, for each column j, compute diagonal L[j][j], then for i>j compute L[i][j]. The sum for each element is independent (same order k=0..j-1). So we can parallelize the i-loop for a fixed j with `#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)` and keep identical per-element summation, hence bit-identical results. But the loop as written is over i outer, j inner. Reorganize to column-oriented: for j = 0..n-1: diag; then parallel for i=j+1..n-1 compute A[i*n+j] = (A[i*n+j]-sum)/A[j*n+j]. Same values, same summation order, so bit-identical. \n\nThis column-oriented parallelization has limited parallelism: at column j, only n-j rows. Fine for large n but synchronization each column. More efficient: blocked right-looking with panel factorization (parallelism over rows within panel) then trailing update (large GEMM-like parallel). For n=512, barriers cost is small relative. Each thread's work varies; static schedule per column: with contiguous i blocks, each has same k count, so balanced. This is basically a rank-1 / column update approach but no blocking: memory access patterns: For each i, accessing row i (A[i*n+k]) contiguous and row j contiguous. Should be reasonably cache-friendly. However parallelizing over i with contiguous chunks: each thread touches different rows. Fine.\n\nBut performance of unblocked column algorithm: total 2 flops per inner iteration * n^3/6 ≈ n^3/3 flops correct. Blocked Cholesky: factor diagonal block, then panel solve, then update trailing with GEMM. The update summation order for element (i,j) in trailing becomes sum over blocks: sum over k-block of dot products, different association than sequential sum over all k. The sequential column algorithm computes each element's dot product at the time its column is processed: L[i][j] = (A[i][j] - sum_{k<j} L[i][k]L[j][k]) / L[j][j], computed in one pass k=0..j-1. {"type":"tool_use","timestamp":1792515320758,"sessionID":"ses_ea5c42782ffe71aBdPEosxd65H","part":{"type":"tool","tool":"write","callID":"call_00_ty6cdxdve0tpa7vyhpq2a99m","state":{"status":"completed","input":{"filePath":"/home/llmtest/evals/20261019-134042/cholesky_deepseek-v4.1-flash-oc-high_omp_r3/cholesky/cholesky.cpp","content":"#include <algorithm>\n#include <chrono>\n#include <cmath>\n#include <cstdio>\n#include <cstdlib>\n#include <cstring>\n#include <vector>\n\n#include <omp.h>\n\n#include \"../common/results_output.hpp\"\n\n// Shared-memory parallel Cholesky decomposition (OpenMP).\n// Decomposes positive definite matrix A into L * L^T where L is lower triangular.\n//\n// The matrix is processed in panels of columns.  For every panel:\n//   1. the small diagonal block is factorized,\n//   2. the panel below the diagonal block is solved (independent rows),\n//   3. the trailing sub-matrix is updated with a rank-block update (independent\n//      rows of the trailing sub-matrix).\n// Steps 2 and 3 contain the vast majority of the work and are distributed over\n// the available cores.  A single parallel region is kept alive across all\n// panels so that worker threads are created only once.\n\nstatic constexpr size_t BLOCK_SIZE = 64;\n\nbool choleskyDecomposition(std::vector<double>& A, const size_t n) {\n    // A is stored in row-major order.\n    double* const base = A.data();\n    bool ok = true;\n\n#pragma omp parallel shared(A, base, n, ok)\n    {\n        for (size_t blockStart = 0; blockStart < n; blockStart += BLOCK_SIZE) {\n            const size_t blockEnd = std::min(blockStart + BLOCK_SIZE, n);\n\n            // ---------------------------------------------------------------\n            // 1. Factorize the diagonal block (small, kept sequential).\n            // ---------------------------------------------------------------\n#pragma omp single\n            {\n                ok = true;\n                for (size_t j = blockStart; j < blockEnd && ok; ++j) {\n                    double* const Aj = base + j * n;\n                    double sum = 0.0;\n#pragma omp simd reduction(+ : sum)\n                    for (size_t p = blockStart; p < j; ++p) {\n                        sum += Aj[p] * Aj[p];\n                    }\n                    const double val = Aj[j] - sum;\n                    if (val <= 0.0) {\n                        printf(\"Error: Matrix is not positive definite at diagonal element %zu\\n\", j);\n                        ok = false;\n                        break;\n                    }\n                    const double diag = std::sqrt(val);\n                    Aj[j] = diag;\n                    for (size_t i = j + 1; i < blockEnd; ++i) {\n                        double* const Ai = base + i * n;\n                        double off = 0.0;\n#pragma omp simd reduction(+ : off)\n                        for (size_t p = blockStart; p < j; ++p) {\n                            off += Ai[p] * Aj[p];\n                        }\n                        Ai[j] = (Ai[j] - off) / diag;\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n            // implicit barrier: all threads observe `ok`\n\n            if (!ok) {\n                break;\n            }\n\n            // ---------------------------------------------------------------\n            // 2. Solve the panel below the diagonal block row by row.\n            // ---------------------------------------------------------------\n#pragma omp for schedule(dynamic, 1)\n            for (size_t i = blockEnd; i < n; ++i) {\n                double* const Ai = base + i * n;\n                for (size_t j = blockStart; j < blockEnd; ++j) {\n                    const double* const Aj = base + j * n;\n                    double sum = 0.0;\n#pragma omp simd reduction(+ : sum)\n                    for (size_t p = blockStart; p < j; ++p) {\n                        sum += Ai[p] * Aj[p];\n                    }\n                    Ai[j] = (Ai[j] - sum) / Aj[j];\n                }\n            }\n            // implicit barrier\n\n            // ---------------------------------------------------------------\n            // 3. Update the trailing sub-matrix: A -= L_panel * L_panel^T.\n            //    Only the lower triangle is touched.\n            // ---------------------------------------------------------------\n#pragma omp for schedule(dynamic, 1)\n            for (size_t i = blockEnd; i < n; ++i) {\n                double* const Ai = base + i * n;\n                size_t j = blockEnd;\n                for (; j + 3 <= i; j += 4) {\n                    const double* const Aj0 = base + j * n;\n                    const double* const Aj1 = base + (j + 1) * n;\n                    const double* const Aj2 = base + (j + 2) * n;\n                    const double* const Aj3 = base + (j + 3) * n;\n                    double s0 = 0.0, s1 = 0.0, s2 = 0.0, s3 = 0.0;\n#pragma omp simd reduction(+ : s0, s1, s2, s3)\n                    for (size_t p = blockStart; p < blockEnd; ++p) {\n                        const double a = Ai[p];\n                        s0 += a * Aj0[p];\n                        s1 += a * Aj1[p];\n                        s2 += a * Aj2[p];\n                        s3 += a * Aj3[p];\n                    }\n                    Ai[j] -= s0;\n                    Ai[j + 1] -= s1;\n                    Ai[j + 2] -= s2;\n                    Ai[j + 3] -= s3;\n                }\n                for (; j <= i; ++j) {\n                    const double* const Aj = base + j * n;\n                    double sum = 0.0;\n#pragma omp simd reduction(+ : sum)\n                    for (size_t p = blockStart; p < blockEnd; ++p) {\n                        sum += Ai[p] * Aj[p];\n                    }\n                    Ai[j] -= sum;\n                }\n            }\n            // implicit barrier\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (!ok) {\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    // Zero out the upper triangular part (the original code guarantees this).\n#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        double* const Ai = base + i * n;\n        for (size_t j = i + 1; j < n; ++j) {\n            Ai[j] = 0.0;\n        }\n    }\n\n    return true;\n}\n\n// Generate a symmetric positive definite matrix\nvoid generatePositiveDefiniteMatrix(std::vector<double>& A, const size_t n) {\n    // Method: Create A = B * B^T where B is random\n    // This guarantees A is positive semi-definite\n    // Then add identity to make it strictly positive definite\n\n    std::vector<double> B(n * n);\n    unsigned int seed = 42;\n\n    // Generate random matrix B\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n * n; ++i) {\n        B[i] = (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 0.5;\n    }\n\n    // Compute A = B * B^T (each entry is computed independently)\n#pragma omp parallel for collapse(2) schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        for (size_t j = 0; j < n; ++j) {\n            double sum = 0.0;\n            const double* const Bi = B.data() + i * n;\n            const double* const Bj = B.data() + j * n;\n            for (size_t k = 0; k < n; ++k) {\n                sum += Bi[k] * Bj[k];\n            }\n            A[i * n + j] = sum;\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Add diagonal dominance to ensure positive definiteness\n#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        A[i * n + i] += n;\n    }\n}\n\nbool validateCholesky(const std::vector<double>& L, const std::vector<double>& A_orig, const size_t n) {\n    // Validate by computing L * L^T and comparing with original matrix\n\n    std::vector<double> reconstructed(n * n);\n\n    // Compute L * L^T\n#pragma omp parallel for collapse(2) schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        for (size_t j = 0; j < n; ++j) {\n            double sum = 0.0;\n            const double* const Li = L.data() + i * n;\n            const double* const Lj = L.data() + j * n;\n            for (size_t k = 0; k < n; ++k) {\n                sum += Li[k] * Lj[k];\n            }\n            reconstructed[i * n + j] = sum;\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Compare with original\n    double maxError = 0.0;\n    double relError = 0.0;\n\n#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static) reduction(max : maxError, relError)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n * n; ++i) {\n        const double error = fabs(reconstructed[i] - A_orig[i]);\n        maxError = std::max(maxError, error);\n\n        const double rel = error / (fabs(A_orig[i]) + 1e-10);\n        relError = std::max(relError, rel);\n    }\n\n    printf(\"Max absolute error: %.10e\\n\", maxError);\n    printf(\"Max relative error: %.10e\\n\", relError);\n\n    // Check if error is within tolerance\n    if (relError > 1e-6) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: relative error too large\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -n <num>     Matrix size (default: 512)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -v           Enable validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -r           Print results for external validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -h           Show this help message\\n\");\n}\n\nint main(int argc, char** argv) {\n    size_t n = 512;\n    bool validate = false;\n    bool printResults = false;\n\n    // Parse command line arguments\n    for (int i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {\n        if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-n\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            n = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-v\") == 0) {\n            validate = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-r\") == 0) {\n            printResults = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-h\") == 0) {\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 0;\n        } else {\n            printf(\"Unknown option: %s\\n\", argv[i]);\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 1;\n        }\n    }\n\n    printf(\"Cholesky Decomposition Benchmark\\n\");\n    printf(\"Matrix size: %zu x %zu\\n\", n, n);\n    printf(\"Validation: %s\\n\", validate ? \"enabled\" : \"disabled\");\n\n    // Allocate matrix\n    std::vector<double> A(n * n);\n    std::vector<double> A_orig;\n\n    // Generate positive definite matrix\n    printf(\"Generating positive definite matrix...\\n\");\n    generatePositiveDefiniteMatrix(A, n);\n\n    if (validate) {\n        A_orig = A; // Save original for validation\n    }\n\n    // Perform Cholesky decomposition\n    printf(\"Computing Cholesky decomposition...\\n\");\n    auto start = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n\n    bool success = choleskyDecomposition(A, n);\n\n    auto end = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n    auto duration = std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(end - start);\n\n    if (!success) {\n        printf(\"Cholesky decomposition failed\\n\");\n        return 1;\n    }\n\n    printf(\"Computation time: %ld ms\\n\", duration.count());\n\n    // Calculate GFLOPS (approximately n³/3 operations for Cholesky)\n    double ops = (double)n * n * n / 3.0;\n    double gflops = ops / (duration.count() / 1000.0) / 1e9;\n    printf(\"Performance: %.3f GFLOPS\\n\", gflops);\n\n    // Print results for external validation\n    if (printResults) {\n        print_results(A, \"CholeskyL\");\n    }\n\n    // Validation\n    if (validate) {\n        printf(\"Validating result...\\n\");\n        bool valid = validateCholesky(A, A_orig, n);\n\n        if (valid) {\n            printf(\"Validation: PASSED\\n\");\n            return 0;\n        } else {\n            printf(\"Validation: FAILED\\n\");\n            return 1;\n        }\n    }\n\n    return 0;\n}\n"},"output":"Wrote file successfully.","metadata":{"diagnostics":{},"filepath":"/home/llmtest/evals/20261019-134042/cholesky_deepseek-v4.1-flash-oc-high_omp_r3/cholesky/cholesky.cpp","exists":true,"truncated":false},"title":"home/llmtest/evals/20261019-134042/cholesky_deepseek-v4.1-flash-oc-high_omp_r3/cholesky/cholesky.cpp","time":{"start":1792515320645,"end":1792515320720}},"id":"prt_15a3d22d6001L1oSUxRyGIJWOk","sessionID":"ses_ea5c42782ffe71aBdPEosxd65H","messageID":"msg_15a3ccfd9001JkdUhkwahLTbkc"}}

{"type":"tool_use","timestamp":1792515429582,"sessionID":"ses_ea5c42782ffe71aBdPEosxd65H","part":{"type":"tool","tool":"edit","callID":"call_00_dqj9kzkewmpiuwna5mrd3hcy","state":{"status":"completed","input":{"filePath":"/home/llmtest/evals/20261019-134042/cholesky_deepseek-v4.1-flash-oc-high_omp_r3/cholesky/cholesky.cpp","oldString":"// Shared-memory parallel Cholesky decomposition (OpenMP).\n// Decomposes positive definite matrix A into L * L^T where L is lower triangular.\n//\n// The matrix is processed in panels of columns.  For every panel:\n//   1. the small diagonal block is factorized,\n//   2. the panel below the diagonal block is solved (independent rows),\n//   3. the trailing sub-matrix is updated with a rank-block update (independent\n//      rows of the trailing sub-matrix).\n// Steps 2 and 3 contain the vast majority of the work and are distributed over\n// the available cores.  A single parallel region is kept alive across all\n// panels so that worker threads are created only once.\n\nstatic constexpr size_t BLOCK_SIZE = 64;\n\nbool choleskyDecomposition(std::vector<double>& A, const size_t n) {\n    // A is stored in row-major order.\n    double* const base = A.data();\n    bool ok = true;\n\n#pragma omp parallel shared(A, base, n, ok)\n    {\n        for (size_t blockStart = 0; blockStart < n; blockStart += BLOCK_SIZE) {\n            const size_t blockEnd = std::min(blockStart + BLOCK_SIZE, n);\n\n            // ---------------------------------------------------------------\n            // 1. Factorize the diagonal block (small, kept sequential).\n            // ---------------------------------------------------------------\n#pragma omp single\n            {\n                ok = true;\n                for (size_t j = blockStart; j < blockEnd && ok; ++j) {\n                    double* const Aj = base + j * n;\n                    double sum = 0.0;\n#pragma omp simd reduction(+ : sum)\n                    for (size_t p = blockStart; p < j; ++p) {\n                        sum += Aj[p] * Aj[p];\n                    }\n                    const double val = Aj[j] - sum;\n                    if (val <= 0.0) {\n                        printf(\"Error: Matrix is not positive definite at diagonal element %zu\\n\", j);\n                        ok = false;\n                        break;\n                    }\n                    const double diag = std::sqrt(val);\n                    Aj[j] = diag;\n                    for (size_t i = j + 1; i < blockEnd; ++i) {\n                        double* const Ai = base + i * n;\n                        double off = 0.0;\n#pragma omp simd reduction(+ : off)\n                        for (size_t p = blockStart; p < j; ++p) {\n                            off += Ai[p] * Aj[p];\n                        }\n                        Ai[j] = (Ai[j] - off) / diag;\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n            // implicit barrier: all threads observe `ok`\n\n            if (!ok) {\n                break;\n            }\n\n            // ---------------------------------------------------------------\n            // 2. Solve the panel below the diagonal block row by row.\n            // ---------------------------------------------------------------\n#pragma omp for schedule(dynamic, 1)\n            for (size_t i = blockEnd; i < n; ++i) {\n                double* const Ai = base + i * n;\n                for (size_t j = blockStart; j < blockEnd; ++j) {\n                    const double* const Aj = base + j * n;\n                    double sum = 0.0;\n#pragma omp simd reduction(+ : sum)\n                    for (size_t p = blockStart; p < j; ++p) {\n                        sum += Ai[p] * Aj[p];\n                    }\n                    Ai[j] = (Ai[j] - sum) / Aj[j];\n                }\n            }\n            // implicit barrier\n\n            // ---------------------------------------------------------------\n            // 3. i < n; ++i) {\n                double* const Ai = base + i * n;\n                size_t j = blockEnd;\n                for (; j + 3 <= i; j += 4) {\n                    const double* const Aj0 = base + j * n;\n                    const double* const Aj1 = base + (j + 1) * n;\n                    const double* const Aj2 = base + (j + 2) * n;\n                    const double* const Aj3 = base + (j + 3) * n;\n                    double s0 = 0.0, s1 = 0.0, s2 = 0.0, s3 = 0.0;\n#pragma omp simd reduction(+ : s0, s1, s2, s3)\n                    for (size_t p = blockStart; p < blockEnd; ++p) {\n                        const double a = Ai[p];\n                        s0 += a * Aj0[p];\n                        s1 += a * Aj1[p];\n                        s2 += a * Aj2[p];\n                        s3 += a * Aj3[p];\n                    }\n                    Ai[j] -= s0;\n                    Ai[j + 1] -= s1;\n                    Ai[j + 2] -= s2;\n                    Ai[j + 3] -= s3;\n                }\n                for (; The sum\n//   R[i][j] = sum_{k<j} L[i][k] * L[j][k]\n// is maintained in a separate accumulator and is built up column by column for\n// increasing k.  Terms are therefore accumulated in exactly the same order as\n// in the sequential reference implementation, which keeps the results\n// bit-for-bit reproducible, while the O(n^3) work of the trailing rank-1\n// update and the O(n^2) column solves are distributed over all cores.\n//\n// The accumulator reuses the input matrix storage: the original matrix is\n// first transposed into column-major order (L) and A is then zeroed and used\n// as R.  Everything is converted back to the original row-major layout at the\n// end, including the zeroed upper triangle.\n\nbool choleskyDecomposition(std::vector<double>& A, const size_t n) {\n    const size_t total = n * n;\n\n    // Column-major copy of the matrix, later holding L (Lc[j*n + i] = L[i][j]).\n    std::vector<double> Lc(total);\n#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t j = 0; j < n; ++j) {\n        const double* const Aj = A.data() + j;\n        double* const Lj = Lc.data() + j * n;\n        for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n            Lj[i] = Aj[i * n];\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Reuse A as the (zero initialized) accumulator R, in column-major order.\n    double* const R = A.data();\n#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < total; ++i) {\n        R[i] = 0.0;\n    }\n\n    double* const L = Lc.data();\n    bool ok = true;\n\n#pragma omp parallel shared(L, R, n, ok)\n    {\n        for (size_t k = 0; k < n; ++k) {\n            // Diagonal element of column k.\n#pragma omp single\n            {\n                const double val = L[k * n + k] - R[k * n + k];\n                if (val <= 0.0) {\n                    printf(\"Error: Matrix is not positive definite at diagonal element %zu\\n\", k);\n                    ok = false;\n                } else {\n                    L[k * n + k] = std::sqrt(val);\n                }\n            }\n            // implicit barrier: every thread sees `ok`\n            if (!ok) {\n                break;\n            }\n\n            // Off-diagonal elements of column k (independent rows).\n            double* const Lk = L + k * n;\n            const double* const Rk = R + k * n;\n            const double diag = Lk[k];\n#pragma omp for schedule(static)\n            for (size_t i = k + 1; i < n; ++i) {\n                Lk[i] = (Lk[i] - Rk[i]) / diag;\n            }\n            // implicit barrier\n\n            // Rank-1 update of the trailing accumulator.  Column j only reads\n            // finalized entries of column k, so all j are independent.  A\n            // cyclic static schedule balances the triangular work evenly.\n#pragma omp for schedule(static, 1)\n            for (size_t j = k + 1; j < n; ++j) {\n                const double lj = Lk[j];\n                double* const Rj = R + j * n;\n#pragma omp simd\n                for (size_t i = j; i < n; ++i) {\n                    Rj[i] += Lk[i] * lj;\n                }\n            }\n            // implicit barrier\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (!ok) {\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    // Convert L back to the original row-major layout and zero the upper part.\n#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        double* const Ai = A.data() + i * n;\n        for (size_t j = 0; j <= i; ++j) {\n            Ai[j] = Lc[j * n + i];\n        }\n        for (size_t j = i + 1; j < n; ++j) {\n            Ai[j] = 0.0;\n        }\n    }\n\n    return true;\n}"},"output":"Edit applied successfully.","metadata":{"diagnostics":{},"diff":"Index: /home/llmtest/evals/20261019-134042/cholesky_deepseek-v4.1-flash-oc-high_omp_r3/cholesky/cholesky.cpp\n===================================================================\n--- /home/llmtest/evals/20261019-134042/cholesky_deepseek-v4.1-flash-oc-high_omp_r3/cholesky/cholesky.cpp\n+++ /home/llmtest/evals/20261019-134042/cholesky_deepseek-v4.1-flash-oc-high_omp_r3/cholesky/cholesky.cpp\n@@ -12,121 +12,85 @@\n \n // Shared-memory parallel Cholesky decomposition (OpenMP).\n // Decomposes positive definite matrix A into L * L^T where L is lower triangular.\n //\n-// The matrix is processed in panels of columns. {"type":"tool_use","timestamp":1792515617649,"sessionID":"ses_ea5c42782ffe71aBdPEosxd65H","part":{"type":"tool","tool":"edit","callID":"call_00_vevvaiso341gt00tsqb108cd","state":{"status":"completed","input":{"filePath":"/home/llmtest/evals/20261019-134042/cholesky_deepseek-v4.1-flash-oc-high_omp_r3/cholesky/cholesky.cpp","oldString":"// Shared-memory parallel Cholesky decomposition (OpenMP).\n// Decomposes positive definite matrix A into L * L^T where L is lower triangular.\n//\n// The algorithm is the classic left-looking column factorization.  The sum\n//   R[i][j] = sum_{k<j} L[i][k] * L[j][k]\n// is maintained in a separate accumulator and is built up column by column for\n// increasing k.  Terms are therefore accumulated in exactly the same order as\n// in the sequential reference implementation, which keeps the results\n// bit-for-bit reproducible, while the O(n^3) work of the trailing rank-1\n// update and the O(n^2) column solves are distributed over all cores.\n//\n// The accumulator reuses the input matrix storage: the original matrix is\n// first transposed into column-major order (L) and A is then zeroed and used\n// as R.  Everything is converted back to the original row-major layout at the\n// end, including the zeroed upper triangle.\n\nbool choleskyDecomposition(std::vector<double>& A, const size_t n) {\n    const size_t total = n * n;\n\n    // Column-major copy of the matrix, later holding L (Lc[j*n + i] = L[i][j]).\n    std::vector<double> Lc(total);\n#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t j = 0; j < n; ++j) {\n        const double* const Aj = A.data() + j;\n        double* const Lj = Lc.data() + j * n;\n        for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n            Lj[i] = Aj[i * n];\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Reuse A as the (zero initialized) accumulator R, in column-major order.\n    double* const R = A.data();\n#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < total; ++i) {\n        R[i] = 0.0;\n    }\n\n    double* const L = Lc.data();\n    bool ok = true;\n\n#pragma omp parallel shared(L, R, n, ok)\n    {\n        for (size_t k = 0; k < n; ++k) {\n            // Diagonal element of column k.\n#pragma omp single\n            {\n                const double val = L[k * n + k] - R[k * n + k];\n                if (val <= 0.0) {\n                    printf(\"Error: Matrix is not positive definite at diagonal element %zu\\n\", k);\n                    ok = false;\n                } else {\n                    L[k * n + k] = std::sqrt(val);\n                }\n            }\n            // implicit barrier: every thread sees `ok`\n            if (!ok) {\n                break;\n            }\n\n            // Off-diagonal elements of column k (independent rows).\n            double* const Lk = L + k * n;\n            const double* const Rk = R + k * n;\n            const double diag = Lk[k];\n#pragma omp for schedule(static)\n            for (size_t i = k + 1; i < n; ++i) {\n                Lk[i] = (Lk[i] - Rk[i]) / diag;\n            }\n            // implicit barrier\n\n            // Rank-1 update of the trailing accumulator.  Column j only reads\n            // finalized entries of column k, so all j are independent.  A\n            // cyclic static schedule balances the triangular work evenly.\n#pragma omp for schedule(static, 1)\n            for (size_t j = k + 1; j < n; ++j) {\n                const double lj = Lk[j];\n                double* const Rj = R + j * n;\n                for (size_t i = j; i < n; ++i) {\n                    Rj[i] = std::fma(Lk[i], lj, Rj[i]);\n                }\n            }\n            // implicit barrier\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (!ok) {\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    // Convert L back to the original row-major layout and zero the upper part.\n#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        double* const Ai = A.data() + i * n;\n        for (size_t j = 0; j <= i; ++j) {\n            Ai[j] = Lc[j * n + i];\n        }\n        for (size_t j = i + 1; j < n; ++j) {\n            Ai[j] = 0.0;\n        }\n    }\n\n    return true;\n}","newString":"// Shared-memory parallel Cholesky decomposition (OpenMP).\n// Decomposes positive definite matrix A into L * L^T where L is lower triangular.\n//\n// The original left-looking formulation is kept: for each column j the entries\n// L[i][j] (i > j) are obtained from the dot product\n//   sum_{k<j} L[i][k] * L[j][k].\n// Those dot products are completely independent of each other, so the rows of\n// a column are distributed over the cores.  Columns are processed one after\n// another (a column depends on all previous ones).  Because every element uses\n// exactly the same dot-product loop as the sequential reference, the produced\n// values are identical; only the loop order across independent elements\n// changes.  A single parallel region is kept alive for the whole factorization\n// so threads are created only once.\n\nbool choleskyDecomposition(std::vector<double>& A, const size_t n) {\n    double* const base = A.data();\n    bool ok = true;\n\n#pragma omp parallel shared(base, n, ok)\n    {\n        for (size_t j = 0; j < n; ++j) {\n            double* const Aj = base + j * n;\n\n            // Diagonal element of column j (cheap, kept sequential).\n#pragma omp single\n            {\n                double sum = 0.0;\n                for (size_t k = 0; k < j; ++k) {\n                    sum += Aj[k] * Aj[k];\n                }\n                const double val = Aj[j] - sum;\n                if (val <= 0.0) {\n                    printf(\"Error: Matrix is not positive definite at diagonal element %zu\\n\", j);\n                    ok = false;\n                } else {\n                    Aj[j] = std::sqrt(val);\n                }\n            }\n            // implicit barrier: every thread observes `ok`\n            if (!ok) {\n                break;\n            }\n\n            const double diag = Aj[j];\n\n            // Off-diagonal elements of column j.  Every row i is independent,\n            // and each row keeps the exact same dot-product order as the\n            // original code.  Equal work per row, so static is optimal.\n#pragma omp for schedule(static)\n            for (size_t i = j + 1; i < n; ++i) {\n                double* const Ai = base + i * n;\n                double sum = 0.0;\n                for (size_t k = 0; k < j; ++k) {\n                    sum += Ai[k] * Aj[k];\n                }\n                Ai[j] = (Ai[j] - sum) / diag;\n            }\n            // implicit barrier\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (!ok) {\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    // Zero out the upper triangular part (the original code guarantees this).\n#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        double* const Ai = base + i * n;\n        for (size_t j = i + 1; j < n; ++j) {\n            Ai[j] = 0.0;\n        }\n    }\n\n    return true;\n}"},"output":"Edit applied successfully.","metadata":{"diagnostics":{},"diff":"Index: /home/llmtest/evals/20261019-134042/cholesky_deepseek-v4.1-flash-oc-high_omp_r3/cholesky/cholesky.cpp\n===================================================================\n--- /home/llmtest/evals/20261019-134042/cholesky_deepseek-v4.1-flash-oc-high_omp_r3/cholesky/cholesky.cpp\n+++ /home/llmtest/evals/20261019-134042/cholesky_deepseek-v4.1-flash-oc-high_omp_r3/cholesky/cholesky.cpp\n@@ -12,84 +12,61 @@\n \n // Shared-memory parallel Cholesky decomposition (OpenMP).\n // Decomposes positive definite matrix A into L * L^T where L is lower triangular.\n //\n-// The algorithm is the classic left-looking column factorization. If we process **blocks of columns** [s,e), we can compute columns within the block sequentially (s..e-1) but each column's row-parallel dot needs previous columns within the block (L[i][k] for k in [s,j)) which are computed in earlier columns of the block. Could parallelize over rows for the entire block using a task per row: For row i >= e, compute all j in [s,e) sequentially using A[i][k] (updated for k in block) and A[j][k] (previous rows/columns). Each row i independent! This is exactly the panel solve in the blocked algorithm: for each row i, solve the lower-triangular system for the block of columns. But the dot sums here use k=0..j-1 in one pass, exactly like original! Yes! We can compute each L[i][j] for j in [s,e) with the full dot over k=0..j-1, preserving exact order, and each row i independent. But this recomputes the sum over k<s for every j in the block, i.e., O(e-s) times more work for the historical part. That's the same as the column algorithm (no update), but blocked to reduce barriers. Total work = sum over blocks and rows of sum_{j in block} j ≈ (n/B)*? Let's compute: for each block [s,e), for each row i>=e, work sum_{j=s}^{e-1} j ≈ B*s (for s>>B). Sum over blocks s=kB: B * (sum over blocks B? ) Let's approximate n rows and n columns: For block at s, rows ~n-s, work per row ~ B*s. Total ~ sum_s (n-s) B s. With s=mB, delta=B: ~ B * ∫ (n-s)s ds = B * n^3/6. So total work is (B) times the original n^3/6! In block version, for each block, rows i>=e only (not i within block), and each j in block uses dot full k<j. Work = sum_{blocks} (n-e) * sum_{j=s}^{e-1} j. Compare original for those columns: sum_{j=s}^{e-1} (n-j) j. Our factor (n-e) vs (n-j); since e > j, (n-e) <= (n-j), actually less! Columns are processed one after\n// another (a column depends on all previous ones).  Because every element uses\n// exactly the same dot-product loop as the sequential reference, the produced\n// values are identical; only the loop order across independent elements\n// changes.  A single parallel region is kept alive for the whole factorization\n// so threads are created only once.\n\nbool choleskyDecomposition(std::vector<double>& A, const size_t n) {\n    double* const base = A.data();\n    bool ok = true;\n\n#pragma omp parallel shared(base, n, ok)\n    {\n        for (size_t j = 0; j < n; ++j) {\n            double* const Aj = base + j * n;\n\n            // Diagonal element of column j (cheap, kept sequential).\n#pragma omp single\n            {\n                double sum = 0.0;\n                for (size_t k = 0; k < j; ++k) {\n                    sum += Aj[k] * Aj[k];\n                }\n                const double val = Aj[j] - sum;\n                if (val <= 0.0) {\n                    printf(\"Error: Matrix is not positive definite at diagonal element %zu\\n\", j);\n                    ok = false;\n                } else {\n                    Aj[j] = std::sqrt(val);\n                }\n            }\n            // implicit barrier: every thread observes `ok`\n            if (!ok) {\n                break;\n            }\n\n            const double diag = Aj[j];\n\n            // Off-diagonal elements of column j.  Every row i is independent,\n            // and each row keeps the exact same dot-product order as the\n            // original code.  Equal work per row, so static is optimal.\n#pragma omp for schedule(static)\n            for (size_t i = j + 1; i < n; ++i) {\n                double* const Ai = base + i * n;\n                double sum = 0.0;\n                for (size_t k = 0; k < j; ++k) {\n                    sum += Ai[k] * Aj[k];\n                }\n                Ai[j] = (Ai[j] - sum) / diag;\n            }\n            // implicit barrier\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (!ok) {\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    // Zero out the upper triangular part (the original code guarantees this).\n#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        double* const Ai = base + i * n;\n        for (size_t j = i + 1; j < n; ++j) {\n            Ai[j] = 0.0;\n        }\n    }\n\n    return true;\n}","newString":"// Shared-memory parallel Cholesky decomposition (OpenMP).\n// Decomposes positive definite matrix A into L * L^T where L is lower triangular.\n//\n// The left-looking formulation of the sequential code is preserved: every\n// entry L[i][j] is still obtained from the exact dot product\n//   sum_{k<j} L[i][k] * L[j][k]\n// evaluated in increasing k order.  Consequently the numerical results are\n// identical to the original implementation.\n//\n// Columns are processed in panels.  For each panel:\n//   1. the small diagonal block is factorized (sequential, since its rows\n//      depend on each other),\n//   2. all rows below the panel are solved independently and distributed over\n//      the cores.  This step contains the O(n^3) bulk of the work.\n// Keeping one parallel region alive for the entire factorization avoids\n// repeated thread creation, and using panels rather than single columns keeps\n// the number of barriers low, which is essential for scalability.\n\nstatic constexpr size_t BLOCK_SIZE = 8;\n\nbool choleskyDecomposition(std::vector<double>& A, const size_t n) {\n    double* const base = A.data();\n    bool ok = true;\n\n#pragma omp parallel shared(base, n, ok)\n    {\n        for (size_t blockStart = 0; blockStart < n; blockStart += BLOCK_SIZE) {\n            const size_t blockEnd = std::min(blockStart + BLOCK_SIZE, n);\n\n            // ---------------------------------------------------------------\n            // 1. Factorize the diagonal block sequentially.\n            // ---------------------------------------------------------------\n#pragma omp single\n            {\n                for (size_t i = blockStart; i < blockEnd && ok; ++i) {\n                    double* const Ai = base + i * n;\n\n                    // Off-diagonal entries of the panel rows.\n                    for (size_t j = blockStart; j < i; ++j) {\n                        const double* const Aj = base + j * n;\n                        double sum = 0.0;\n                        for (size_t k = 0; k < j; ++k) {\n                            sum += Ai[k] * Aj[k];\n                        }\n                        Ai[j] = (Ai[j] - sum) / Aj[j];\n                    }\n\n                    // Diagonal entry.\n                    double sum = 0.0;\n                    for (size_t k = 0; k < i; ++k) {\n                        sum += Ai[k] * Ai[k];\n                    }\n                    const double val = Ai[i] - sum;\n                    if (val <= 0.0) {\n                        printf(\"Error: Matrix is not positive definite at diagonal element %zu\\n\", i);\n                        ok = false;\n                        break;\n                    }\n                    Ai[i] = std::sqrt(val);\n                }\n            }\n            // implicit barrier: all threads observe `ok`\n            if (!ok) {\n                break;\n            }\n\n            // ---------------------------------------------------------------\n            // 2. Solve the panel below the diagonal block.  Rows are fully\n            //    independent; within a row the columns of the panel are\n            //    solved in order, each with the exact reference dot product.\n            // ---------------------------------------------------------------\n#pragma omp for schedule(static)\n            for (size_t i = blockEnd; i < n; ++i) {\n                double* const Ai = base + i * n;\n                for (size_t j = blockStart; j < blockEnd; ++j) {\n                    const double* const Aj = base + j * n;\n                    double sum = 0.0;\n                    for (size_t k = 0; k < j; ++k) {\n                        sum += Ai[k] * Aj[k];\n                    }\n                    Ai[j] = (Ai[j] - sum) / Aj[j];\n                }\n            }\n            // implicit barrier\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (!ok) {\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    // Zero out the upper triangular part (the original code guarantees this).\n#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        double* const Ai = base + i * n;\n        for (size_t j = i + 1; j < n; ++j) {\n            Ai[j] = 0.0;\n        }\n    }\n\n    return true;\n}"},"output":"Edit applied successfully.","metadata":{"diagnostics":{},"diff":"Index: /home/llmtest/evals/20261019-134042/cholesky_deepseek-v4.1-flash-oc-high_omp_r3/cholesky/cholesky.cpp\n===================================================================\n--- /home/llmtest/evals/20261019-134042/cholesky_deepseek-v4.1-flash-oc-high_omp_r3/cholesky/cholesky.cpp\n+++ /home/llmtest/evals/20261019-134042/cholesky_deepseek-v4.1-flash-oc-high_omp_r3/cholesky/cholesky.cpp\n@@ -12,61 +12,87 @@\n \n // Shared-memory parallel Cholesky decomposition (OpenMP).\n // Decomposes positive definite matrix A into L * L^T where L is lower triangular.\n //\n-// The original left-looking formulation is kept: for each column j the entries\n-// L[i][j] (i > j) are obtained from the dot product\n-//   sum_{k<j} L[i][k] * L[j][k].\n-// Those dot products are completely independent of each other, so the rows of\n-// a column are distributed over the cores. Consequently the numerical results are\n// identical to the original implementation.\n//\n// Columns are processed in panels.  For each panel:\n//   1. the small diagonal block is factorized (sequential, since its rows\n//      depend on each other),\n//   2. all rows below the panel are solved independently and distributed over\n//      the cores.  This step contains the O(n^3) bulk of the work.\n// Keeping one parallel region alive for the entire factorization avoids\n// repeated thread creation, and using panels rather than single columns keeps\n// the number of barriers low, which is essential for scalability.\n\nstatic constexpr size_t BLOCK_SIZE = 8;\n\nbool choleskyDecomposition(std::vector<double>& A, const size_t n) {\n    double* const base = A.data();\n    bool ok = true;\n\n#pragma omp parallel\n    {\n        for (size_t blockStart = 0; blockStart < n; blockStart += BLOCK_SIZE) {\n            const size_t blockEnd = std::min(blockStart + BLOCK_SIZE, n);\n\n            // ---------------------------------------------------------------\n            // 1.
